m_{l},n_{l}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\begin{split}X^{l}(B)\subset B_{\mathcal{W}^{m_{l}}}(0,n_{l}).\end{split}

(2.43)

Next we show that
X^{l}B
is bounded in
\mathcal{M}_{+}
for some
l>0
.
Otherwise, there exist sequences
F_{n}\in B
,
(0,1)\ni l_{n}\to 0
and
\varphi_{n}\in\mathscr{D}_{(0,l_{n})}
such that
\|\varphi_{n}\|_{0}\leq 2^{-n}
and
F_{n}(\varphi_{n})>2
.
Then for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
N_{m}\in C_{+}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
satisfying

N_{m}(l_{n})\|\varphi_{n}\|_{m}\leq 2^{-m-n}

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, which is possible because of
l_{n}\to 0
.
For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
G_{0}\in\bar{B}_{\mathcal{M}_{+}}(0,1)
,
G_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\geq 1}
and
G_{2}\in\mathscr{B}(N)
, we have

\begin{split}&|G_{0}(\varphi_{n})|\leq 2^{-n},\quad G_{1}(\varphi_{n})=0,\quad|G_{2}(\varphi_{n})|\leq\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}N_{m}(l_{n})\|\varphi_{n}\|_{m}\leq 2^{-n}.\end{split}

(2.44)

Hence
|G(\varphi_{n})|\leq 2^{1-n}
for any
G\in\mathscr{B}(1,1,N)
, so
F_{n}\not\in 2^{n}\mathscr{B}(1,1,N)
, contradicting the boundedness of
\{F_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset B
in
\mathcal{X}
.
Hence there exist
\delta,C_{*}\in(0,\infty)
such that
X^{\delta}B\subset B_{\mathcal{M}_{+}}(0,C_{*})
.
For
l>0
and
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{<l}
, we have, using (
2.43
) and Lemma
2.3
,

\begin{split}|F(\varphi)|\leq|F(X^{\delta}\varphi)|+|F(X_{\delta}\varphi)|&\leq C_{*}\|\varphi\|_{0}^{+}+n_{2l}M^{\chi}_{\delta}(m_{k})\|\varphi\|_{m_{2l}}^{+}.\end{split}

(2.45)

There exist
\mu\in C_{\delta}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
and
\nu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
satisfying
\nu(0)\geq C_{*}
and

\begin{split}\mu(l)\geq m_{2k},\quad\nu(l)\geq C_{*}+M^{\chi}_{\delta}(m_{k})n_{2k}\end{split}

(2.46)

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
and
\max(\delta,k-1)<l\leq k
. Then we have
\rho^{\mu(l)}_{l}(F)\leq\nu(l)
for all
F\in B
.

Conversely, suppose that such
\mu\in C_{\delta}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
and
\nu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
exist.
Let
\delta>0
such that
\mu(2\delta)<1
.
There is
\tilde{\nu}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
satisfying
M^{\chi}_{\delta}(\mu(l))\nu(l)\leq\tilde{\nu}(l)
for
l\geq\delta
.
Then Lemma
2.3
implies that
X_{\delta}B
is bounded in
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\tilde{\nu}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{X}
, and
that
X^{\delta}B
is bounded in
\mathcal{M}_{+}
, so it is absorbed by any
B_{\mathcal{M}_{+}}(0,R_{0})\subset\mathscr{B}(R_{0},R_{1},N)\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathcal{X}}
, meaning the boundedness in
\mathcal{X}
. Hence
B=X^{\delta}B+X_{\delta}B
is also bounded in
\mathcal{X}
.
∎

2.4.
Compact sets in
\mathcal{X}

Here we characterize compact subsets of
\mathcal{X}
.
First we recall variant notions of compactness.
A topological space
X
is sequentially compact iff every sequence has a convergent subsequence.

X
is countably compact iff every sequence has an accumulation point.
Countable compactness is weaker both than compactness and than sequential compactness, but there is no order in general between the latter two.

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
be topologies on a set
X
.
Let
\tau_{1}
be countably compact and stronger than
\tau_{2}
.
Let
\tau_{2}
be Hausdorff and sequentially compact.
Then so is
\tau_{1}
.

In particular, in any topological vector space embedded into
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
, countably compact subsets are sequentially compact.

Proof.

The Hausdorff property transfers to the stronger topology, so it suffices to show the sequential compactness of
\tau_{1}
.
For any sequence
S_{0}
, there is a subsequence
S_{1}\subset S_{0}
convergent to some
x_{\infty}
in
\tau_{2}
.
If
S_{1}
is not convergent to
x_{\infty}
in
\tau_{1}
, then there is a
\tau_{1}
-neighborhood
U
of
x_{\infty}
such that
S_{2}:=S_{1}\setminus U
is a subsequence, which has a
\tau_{1}
-accumulation point
x_{*}
.
Since
S_{2}\cap U=\varnothing
, we have
x_{*}\not=x_{\infty}
.
Then
S_{2}\to x_{\infty}
and accumulating at
x_{*}\not=x_{\infty}
in
\tau_{2}
,
contradicting the Hausdorff. Hence
S_{1}\to x_{\infty}
in
\tau_{1}
.
∎

Next we consider the compactness in
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
.

Lemma 2.9
.

K\subset\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
is countably compact iff it is compact in some
\mathcal{W}^{m}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{W}^{m}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
by definition, it suffices to show the necessity.
For the radial mollifiers
\zeta_{n}=n^{d}\zeta(n\xi)
, we have

\begin{split}&|\zeta_{n}*F(\varphi)|\leq\|F\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}\|\zeta_{n}*\varphi\|_{m}\leq\|F\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}n^{m}\|\zeta\|_{W^{1,m}}\|\varphi\|_{0},\\
&|(F-\zeta_{n}*F)(\varphi)|\leq\|F\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}\|\varphi-\zeta_{n}*\varphi\|_{m}\leq\|F\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}n^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{m+1},\end{split}

(2.47)

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}
,
F\in\mathcal{W}^{m}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In particular,
\zeta_{n}*:\mathcal{W}^{m}\to\mathcal{W}^{0}
is bounded for all
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, so is
\zeta_{n}*:\mathcal{W}^{\infty}\to\mathcal{W}^{0}
by definition of the inductive limit.

Let
K\subset\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
be countably compact. Since
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
and bounded subsets of
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
are precompact, the above lemma implies that
K
is sequentially compact. Also, the regularity of
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
yields some
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
R<\infty
such that
K\subset B:=\bar{B}_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}(0,R)
.

To show that
K
is compact in
\mathcal{W}^{m+1}
, take any sequence
\{F_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\subset K
.
Since
K
is sequentially compact in
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
, we may replace it by its subsequence convergent to some
F_{\infty}
in
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
. Let
F_{n}^{\prime}:=F_{n}-F_{\infty}
.
Since
\varphi_{k}*:\mathcal{W}^{\infty}\to\mathcal{W}^{0}
is continuous for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
\|\zeta_{k}*F_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m+1}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, while by the above estimate,

\begin{split}\|F_{n}^{\prime}-\zeta_{k}*F_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m+1}}\leq\|F_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}k^{-1}\leq 2Rk^{-1}\to 0\end{split}

(2.48)

as
k\to\infty
uniformly for
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus we obtain
\|F_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m+1}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, and so
K
is compact in
\mathcal{W}^{m+1}
.
∎

It turns out that every compact subset of
\mathcal{X}
is bounded in a single
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
, but the compactness does not hold for that norm, losing regularity.
By interpolation, the loss may be chosen arbitrarily small, but to estimate convolutions, it is more convenient to use the derivatives as follows. For any
\mu,\nu,\iota\in C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
, let

\begin{split}&\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}:=\{F_{0}+\sum_{1\leq k\leq d}\partial_{k}F_{k}\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\mid F_{0}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu},\ F_{1},\ldots,F_{d}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\iota}\},\\
&\qquad\|G\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}}:=\inf\{\rho^{\mu,\nu}(F_{0})+\sum_{1\leq k\leq d}\rho^{\mu,\iota}(F_{k})\mid G=F_{0}+\sum_{1\leq k\leq d}\partial_{k}F_{k}\}.\end{split}

(2.49)

As shown below, the weight
\iota
is arbitrary in characterizing the compactness,
but its freedom is important for boundedness of multipliers with singularity at the boundary.

For any
G\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}
,
l\in(0,\infty)
and
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{<l}
, we have

\begin{split}|G(\varphi)|&\leq|F_{0}(\varphi)|+\sum_{1\leq k\leq d}|F_{k}(\partial_{k}\varphi)|\\
&\leq\max(\nu(l),\iota(l))\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)+1}[\rho^{\mu,\nu}(F_{0})+\sum_{1\leq k\leq d}\rho^{\mu,\iota}(F_{k})],\end{split}

(2.50)

where
F_{0},\ldots,F_{d}
are as in the above definition. Hence

\begin{split}\rho^{\mu+1,\max(\nu,\iota)}(G)\leq\|G\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}}\end{split}

(2.51)

and in particular
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
is Banach and the representation
F=F_{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{d}\partial_{k}F_{k}
is preserved when each
F_{k}
converges in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
, we deduce that
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}
is also Banach.
By definition, we also have

\begin{split}\|F\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}}\leq\rho^{\mu,\nu}(F),\quad\max_{1\leq k\leq d}\|\partial_{k}F\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}}\leq\rho^{\mu,\iota}(F).\end{split}

(2.52)

Proposition 2.10
.

For any subset
K\subset\mathcal{X}
, the following conditions are equivalent.

(1)

K
is compact in
\mathcal{X}
.

(2)

K
is sequentially compact in
\mathcal{X}
.

(3)

K
is countably compact in
\mathcal{X}
.

(4)

K
is bounded in
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
and compact in
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}
for some
\mu,\nu\in C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
and all
\iota\in C_{+}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
.

(5)

K
is bounded in
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
and compact in the
\rho^{\mu+1,\nu}
norm for some
\mu,\nu\in C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
.

Proof.

(3) follows both from (1) and from (2) in general. Moreover, since
\mathcal{X}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
, by Lemma
2.8
, (3) implies (2).
(4) implies (5) because of (
2.51
).

Next we show that (5) implies (1).
Let
R<\infty
such that
K\subset B:=\bar{B}_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}}(0,R)
. Then it suffices to show that
B\subset\mathcal{X}
is continuous for the
\rho^{\mu+1,\nu}
metric on
B
.
Take any convergent sequence
B\ni F_{n}\to F_{\infty}\in B
and any
U:=\mathscr{B}(R_{0},R_{1},N)\in\mathscr{B}_{\mathcal{X}}
.
Let
F_{n}^{\prime}:=F_{n}-F_{\infty}
. There exists
\delta>0
such that
\mu(2\delta)<1
and
\nu(2\delta)<\tfrac{R_{0}}{4R}
.
Then for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\rho^{\mu,\nu}(F_{n}^{\prime})<2R
implies
X^{\delta}F_{n}^{\prime}\in B_{\mathcal{M}_{+}}(0,R_{0}/2)\subset U/2
.
There exist
\mu_{1},\nu_{1}\in C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
such that
\mu(l)+1\leq\mu_{1}(l)
and
M^{\chi}_{\delta}(\mu(l)+1)\nu(l)\leq\nu_{1}(l)
for
l\geq\delta
.
Then Lemma
2.3
implies
\rho^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1}}(X_{\delta}F_{n}^{\prime})\leq\rho^{\mu+1,\nu}(F_{n}^{\prime})\to 0
,
so
X_{\delta}F_{n}^{\prime}\to 0
in
\mathcal{X}
. Thus
X_{\delta}F_{n}^{\prime}\in U/2
and
F_{n}^{\prime}\in U
for large
n
.
Hence
B\subset\mathcal{X}
is continuous for
\rho^{\mu+1,\nu}
, so
K
is compact also in
\mathcal{X}
.

It remains to show that (2) implies (4). Let
K\subset\mathcal{X}
be sequentially compact.
Proposition
2.7
yields
\mu\in C_{\delta}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
with some
\delta>0
and
\nu_{0}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
such that
\rho^{\mu(l)}_{l}(F)\leq\nu_{0}(l)
for all
F\in K
and
l>0
.

Let
S(l):=\sup_{F\in K}\|X^{l}F\|_{\mathcal{M}}
for
l>0

and suppose that
S(l)\not\to 0
as
l\to+0
.
Then there exist
\varepsilon>0
and sequences
F_{n}\in K
,
(0,\delta)\ni l_{n}\to 0
, and
\varphi_{n}\in\mathscr{D}_{<l_{n}}
such that
\|\varphi_{n}\|_{0}\leq 1
and
F_{n}(\varphi_{n})>\varepsilon
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
By the sequential compactness of
K
, we may assume that
F_{n}
converges to some
F_{\infty}
in
\mathcal{X}
.
There exists
\{N_{m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}\subset C_{+}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
satisfying

N_{m}(l_{n})\|\varphi_{n}\|_{m}\leq 2^{-m-3}\varepsilon

for all
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
. For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
G_{0}\in\bar{B}_{\mathcal{M}_{+}}(0,\varepsilon/4)
,
G_{1}\in\mathcal{X}_{\geq 1}
and
G_{2}\in\mathscr{B}(N)
, we have

\begin{split}&|G_{0}(\varphi_{n})|\leq\varepsilon/4,\quad|G_{1}(\varphi_{n})|=0,\quad|G_{2}(\varphi_{n})|\leq\sum_{m\in\mathbb{N}}N_{m}(l_{n})\|\varphi_{n}\|_{m}\leq\varepsilon/4,\end{split}

(2.53)

so
|G(\varphi_{n})|\leq\varepsilon/2
for any
G\in\mathscr{B}(\varepsilon/4,1,N)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.
On the other hand,
F_{\infty}\in\mathcal{X}
and
l_{n}\to+0
imply
F_{\infty}(\varphi_{n})\to 0
, hence
|(F_{n}-F_{\infty})(\varphi_{n})|>\varepsilon/2
for large
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then
F_{n}-F_{\infty}\not\in\mathscr{B}(\varepsilon/4,1,N)
, contradicting
F_{n}\to F_{\infty}
in
\mathcal{X}
.
Hence
S(l)\to 0
as
l\to+0
, so that we may find
\nu_{1}\in C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
such that
K\subset B_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu_{1}}}(0,1)
.

Let
\nu\in C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
such that
\nu_{1}\leq\nu
,
\nu_{1}(l)M^{\chi}_{l}(\mu(l))/\nu(l)\to 0
as
l\to\infty
, and
\nu_{1}(l)/\nu(l)\to 0
as
l\to+0
.
Take any sequence
F_{n}\in K
convergent to some
F_{\infty}\in K
in
\mathcal{X}
.
It suffices to prove
F_{n}\to F_{\infty}
also in
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}
for all
\iota\in C_{+}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
.
Since we have, using Lemma
2.3
,

\begin{split}&\rho^{\mu,\nu}(X_{H}F)+\rho^{\mu,\nu}(X^{L}F)\leq\rho^{\mu,\nu_{1}}(F)\left[\sup_{l\geq H}\tfrac{\nu_{1}(l)M^{\chi}_{l}(\mu(l))}{\nu(l)}+\sup_{0<l\leq 2L}\tfrac{\nu_{1}(l)}{\nu(l)}\right]\to 0\end{split}

(2.54)

as
H\to\infty
and
\delta\geq L\to+0
uniformly for all
F\in K
, it suffices to show that

F_{n}^{\prime}:=X_{L}X^{H}(F_{n}-F_{\infty})\to 0
in
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}
for all
0<L<\delta<1<H<\infty
.

Since
X^{H}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
is continuous,
X^{H}(K)
is also countably compact in
\mathcal{W}^{\infty}
.
Then by Lemma
2.9
, there exists
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that
X^{H}(K)
is compact in
\mathcal{W}^{m}
. Since
F_{n}\to F_{\infty}
in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
, we deduce that

\|F_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}\to 0
, while
\{F_{n}^{\prime}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is bounded in
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,1}
.
Let
\{\varphi_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be the mollifier in Proposition
2.6
and
\varphi_{n}^{\dagger}(\xi):=\varphi_{n}(-\xi)
. Then

\begin{split}|\varphi_{k}*F_{n}^{\prime}(\varphi)|&\leq\|F_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}\|\varphi_{k}^{\dagger}*X_{L/2}\varphi\|_{m}\\
&\leq\|F_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}r_{k}^{-m}\|\zeta\|_{W^{1,m}}M^{\chi}_{L/2}(\mu(L))\|\varphi\|_{\mu(L)}^{+},\end{split}

(2.55)

for any
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}
, while
\varphi_{k}*F_{n}^{\prime}(\varphi)=0
if
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{<l}
for some
l\leq L
.
Hence if we choose
k=k(n)\to\infty
sufficiently slowly compared with
\|F_{n}^{\prime}\|_{\mathcal{W}^{m}}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
, then
\rho^{\mu,\nu}(\varphi_{k}*F_{n}^{\prime})\to 0
.
On the other hand, we have

\begin{split}&F_{n}^{\prime}-\varphi_{k}*F_{n}^{\prime}=\nabla\cdot F^{\prime}_{k,n},\quad F^{\prime}_{k,n}:=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{|y-c_{k}|<r_{k}}y\varphi_{k}(y)F_{n}^{\prime}(x-\theta y)dyd\theta,\end{split}

(2.56)

and for any
l\in(0,\infty)
, using that
\operatorname{supp}F_{n}^{\prime}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}_{>L}
,

\begin{split}\rho^{\mu(l)}_{l}(F^{\prime}_{k,n})\lesssim(|c_{k}|+r_{k})\rho^{\mu(l)}_{l}(F_{n}^{\prime})\lesssim\tfrac{|c_{k}|+r_{k}}{\iota(L)}\iota(l)\rho^{\mu,1}(F_{n}^{\prime}).\end{split}

(2.57)

Hence
\rho^{\mu,\iota}(F^{\prime}_{k,n})\to 0
as
k(n)\to\infty
and
n\to\infty
.
Thus we obtain
\|F_{n}^{\prime}\|_{X^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}}\to 0
, hence
F_{n}\to F_{\infty}
in
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Remark 2.2
.

Applying the above result to any sequence
\{F_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}}\subset\mathcal{X}
, we obtain characterization of convergence:
F_{n}\to F_{\infty}
in
\mathcal{X}
iff

\begin{split}\sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}(F_{n})<\infty,\quad\lim_{n\to\infty}\|F_{n}-F_{\infty}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}}=0,\end{split}

(2.58)

for some
\mu,\nu\in C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
and all
\iota\in C_{+}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
.
The latter suffices to hold for one
\iota\in C_{+}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
, and also the norm may be replaced with
\rho^{\mu+1,\nu}
.

It is natural to look for simpler characterizations. On one hand, the convergence in any
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
is too strong.
A simple counter-example is
F_{n}(\xi):=\xi_{1}e^{in\xi_{1}}
. It is bounded in
\mathcal{X}^{0,\nu}
with
\nu(l):=l
, and
F_{n}=in^{-1}[(e^{in\xi_{1}}-1)-\partial_{1}\xi_{1}(e^{in\xi_{1}}-1)]
implies
\rho^{1,\nu}(F_{n})\to 0
, hence
F_{n}\to 0
in
\mathcal{X}
, whereas
\|F_{n}\|_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{<l})}=\|F_{1}\|_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{<l})}>0
for all
l>0
implies
\inf_{n}\|F_{n}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}}>0
for any choice of
\mu,\nu\in C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
.
On the other hand, the bounded weak-* convergence is too weak:
\sup_{n}\|F_{n}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}}<\infty
and
F_{n}\to F_{\infty}
in
\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
do not imply
F_{n}\to F_{\infty}
in
\mathcal{X}
, if
d\geq 2
. A simple counter-example is
F_{n}(\xi):=\varphi(\xi-(0,nv))
for any non-zero
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{>0}
and
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}
.
Another candidate is the weak convergence in a single
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
, but it does not seem convenient to handle.

The multiplication factor
\kappa
is essential for the following multiplier estimate. Let

\begin{split}Y:=C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{>0};\mathbb{C})\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{(0,1)};\mathbb{C})\cap\bigcap_{m\geq 0}\bigcap_{n\geq 2}W^{m,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{(1/n,n)};\mathbb{C}).\end{split}

(2.59)

It consists of smooth functions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{>0}
that are bounded as
|\xi^{\perp}|\to\infty
, including all the derivatives, locally uniformly for
0<\xi_{1}<\infty
, and also bounded (but not derivatives) uniformly as
\xi_{1}\to+0
.
Y
is a Fréchet space with the topology defined by those seminorms of
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{(0,1)})
and
W^{m,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{(1/n,n)})
. Thanks to (
2.23
), for any
F\in\mathcal{X}
and
f\in Y
we may define the product
fF\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
by

(fF)(\varphi):=\mathscr{E}\mathscr{R}F(f\varphi)
for all
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
It is indeed bounded on
\mathcal{X}
as follows.

Lemma 2.11
.

Let
n_{1},n_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
and
A:\mathbb{R}^{d}_{>0}\to M(n_{1},n_{2})
with all components in
Y
.
Then for any
\delta>0
and
\mu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}
, there exist
\beta,\gamma\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
such that

\displaystyle\|A^{\intercal}\varphi\|_{\mu(l),\delta}\leq\beta(l)\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+},

(2.60)

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\gamma(2^{2-k}\delta)\|(\nabla A^{\intercal})\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(1<2^{k}\xi_{1}/\delta<4)}+\gamma(l)\|X_{\delta}(\nabla A^{\intercal})\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}\leq\beta(l)\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+},

(2.61)

for all
l\in(0,\infty)
and
\varphi\in(\mathscr{D}_{<l})^{n_{1}}
.

(\beta,\gamma)
may be chosen uniformly for a set of
A
whose components are bounded in
Y
.

Moreover, for any
\beta\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
satisfying (
2.60
) and any
\nu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
, the multiplication with
A
is bounded
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}\to\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\beta\nu}
. If in addition
\gamma\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
satisfies (
2.61
) then it is also bounded
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\gamma\nu}_{\partial}\to\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\beta\nu,\beta\gamma\nu}_{\partial}
. The operator norm is bounded respectively as

\begin{split}\|AF\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\beta\nu}}\leq\|F\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}},\quad\|AF\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\beta\nu,\beta\gamma\nu}_{\partial}}\leq 2\|F\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\gamma\nu}_{\partial}}.\end{split}

(2.62)

Proof.

Using the Leibniz rule and Lemma
2.3
, we have

\begin{split}\|A^{\intercal}\varphi\|_{\mu(l),\delta}&\leq\|A^{\intercal}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{(0,l)};M(n_{2},n_{1}))}\|\varphi\|_{0}^{+}\\
&\quad+2^{\mu(l)}\sup_{|\alpha|\leq\mu(l)}\|\partial^{\alpha}A^{\intercal}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{(\delta,l)};M(n_{2},n_{1}))}M^{\chi}_{\delta}(\mu(l))\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+},\end{split}

(2.63)

where the factors multiplied with the seminorms of
\varphi
are non-increasing and locally bounded in
l\in(0,\infty)
, so that we may find
\beta_{0}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
dominating the sum of them. Then we have

\begin{split}\|A^{\intercal}\varphi\|_{\mu(l),\delta}\leq\beta_{0}(l)\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+}.\end{split}

(2.64)

Similarly, there exist
\{a_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[1,\infty)
and
\beta_{1}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
such that

\begin{split}\|(\nabla A^{\intercal})\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(1<2^{k}\xi_{1}/\delta<4)}\leq a_{k}\|\varphi\|_{0}^{+},\quad\|X_{\delta}(\nabla A^{\intercal})\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}\leq\beta_{1}(l)\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+}.\end{split}

(2.65)

Then we may find
\gamma\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
satisfying

\gamma(2^{2-k}\delta)a_{k}\leq 2^{-1-k}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
\gamma(l)=\gamma(2\delta)\leq 1/4
for all
l\geq 2\delta
.
Choosing
\beta:=\max(1,\beta_{0},\beta_{1})
satisfies both (
2.60
) and (
2.61
).

Next we prove the boundedness assuming (
2.60
). Combining it with (
2.10
) yields

\begin{split}|AF(\varphi)|=|F(A^{\intercal}\varphi)|\leq\nu(l)\rho^{\mu,\nu}_{l}(F)\|A^{\intercal}\varphi\|_{\mu(l),\delta}\leq\nu(l)\rho^{\mu,\nu}_{l}(F)\beta(l)\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+},\end{split}

(2.66)

for all
F\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
,
l>0
and
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{<l}
.
Hence
\rho^{\mu,\beta\nu}_{l}(AF)\leq\rho^{\mu,\nu}_{l}(F)
.

Suppose further (
2.61
). For any
F\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\gamma\nu}_{\partial}
, let
F=F_{0}+\sum_{k}\partial_{k}F_{k}
.
For the first two terms in

AF=AF_{0}+\sum_{k}\partial_{k}(AF_{k})-\sum_{k}(\partial_{k}A)F_{k}
,
we have, using the above estimate,

\begin{split}\rho^{\mu,\beta\nu}(AF_{0})\leq\rho^{\mu,\nu}(F_{0}),\quad\rho^{\mu,\beta\gamma\nu}(AF_{k})\leq\rho^{\mu,\gamma\nu}(F_{k}).\end{split}

(2.67)

For the remaining term, let
X_{\delta}^{(k)}:=X_{2^{-k}\delta}-X_{2^{1-k}\delta}
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
X_{\delta}^{(0)}:=X_{\delta}
.
Using (
2.61
), we have for any
l>0
and
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{<l}
,

\begin{split}&|(\partial_{k}A)F_{k}(\varphi)|=|\mathscr{E}\partial_{k}A\mathscr{R}F_{k}(\varphi)|\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}|F_{k}(X_{\delta}^{(k)}\partial_{k}A^{\intercal}\varphi)|\\
&\leq\rho^{\mu,\gamma\nu}(F_{k})\nu(l)\left[\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\gamma(2^{2-k}\delta)\|X_{\delta}^{(k)}\partial_{k}A^{\intercal}\varphi\|_{0}+\gamma(l)\|X_{\delta}\partial_{k}A^{\intercal}\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+}\right],\\
&\leq\rho^{\mu,\gamma\nu}(F_{k})\nu(l)\beta(l)\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+}.\end{split}

(2.68)

Thus we obtain
\rho^{\mu,\beta\nu}((\partial_{k}A)F_{k})\leq\rho^{\mu,\gamma\nu}(F_{k})
.
Summing the above three estimates yields

\begin{split}\|AF\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\beta\nu,\beta\gamma\nu}_{\partial}}\leq\rho^{\mu,\nu}(F_{0})+2\sum_{1\leq k\leq d}\rho^{\mu,\gamma\nu}(F_{k}),\end{split}

(2.69)

and then the infimum over
F_{0},\ldots,F_{d}
leads to the desired operator bound.
∎

3.
Convolution for the distributions

The purpose of the following analysis is to quantify the propagation of positive frequency by convolution as well as its decay near the zero frequency, in the distribution sense.
For
F,G\in\mathcal{X}
, the convolution is formally defined by

\begin{split}\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\ni\forall\varphi,\quad(F*G)(\varphi):=F_{\xi}(G_{\eta}(\varphi(\xi+\eta))),\end{split}

(3.1)

where the subscript
\eta
of
G_{\eta}
indicates the independent variable of the test function.
In general, for any
G\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we have
G_{\eta}(\varphi(\xi+\eta))\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\begin{split}\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\varphi(\xi+\eta))=G_{\eta}(\partial^{\alpha}\varphi(\xi+\eta)),\end{split}

(3.2)

but we need some condition for
F\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}
to act on it.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
l>0
,
m\geq 0
,
G\in\mathscr{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\rho_{l}^{m}(G)<\infty
and
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{<l}
.
Then we have
G_{\eta}(\varphi(\xi+\eta))\in\mathcal{B}^{\infty}_{+<l}
and

\begin{split}\forall n\in\mathbb{N},\quad\|G_{\eta}(\varphi(\xi+\eta))\|_{n}^{+}\leq\rho_{l}^{m}(G)\|\varphi\|_{m+n}^{+}.\end{split}

(3.3)

Proof.

Since
\operatorname{supp}\varphi
is compact, we have

l^{\prime}:=\max\{\xi_{1}\mid\xi\in\operatorname{supp}\varphi\}<l
.
Then for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{>l^{\prime}}
, we have
\operatorname{supp}_{\eta}\varphi(\xi+\eta)\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}_{<0}
, so
\operatorname{supp}G\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\geq 0}
implies
f(\xi):=G_{\eta}(\varphi(\xi+\eta))=0
.
Hence
\operatorname{supp}f\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}_{\leq l^{\prime}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}_{<l}
. Moreover,
f\in C^{\infty}
and for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}_{>0}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
with
|\alpha|\leq n
we have

\begin{split}|\partial^{\alpha}_{\xi}f(\xi)|=|G_{\eta}(\partial^{\alpha}\varphi(\xi+\eta))|\leq\rho_{l}^{m}(G)\|\partial^{\alpha}\varphi\|_{m}^{+}\leq\rho_{l}^{m}(G)\|\varphi\|_{m+n}^{+}.\end{split}

(3.4)

The supremum for
\xi
and
\alpha
yields the desired estimate.
∎

The above lemma makes sense of (
3.1
) for
F,G\in\mathcal{X}
, where
F
is the extension by Lemma
2.1
to
\mathcal{B}_{+<l}^{\infty}
if
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{<l}
.
This definition is compatible with the convolution for sign-definite locally integrable
F,G
.
Moreover, we have the following estimate on
F*G
.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}
and
\nu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
.
Then for any
F,G\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
,
l\in(0,\infty)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta/2]
and
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{<l}
, we have

\begin{split}\tfrac{|F*G(\varphi)|}{\rho^{\mu,\nu}_{l}(F)\rho^{\mu,\nu}_{l}(G)}\leq 3[\nu(2\varepsilon)\nu(l)&+1_{l>2\varepsilon}\nu(\min(2\delta,l/2))\nu(l-\varepsilon)]\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+}\\
&+1_{l>4\delta}[M_{\delta}^{\chi}(\mu(l-\delta))\nu(l-\delta)]^{2}\|\varphi\|_{2\mu(l-\delta)}^{+}.\end{split}

(3.5)

Proof.

First we consider the case of
l\leq 4\delta
. For any
\varphi\in\mathscr{D}_{<l}
, there is
l^{\prime}\in(0,l)
such that
\operatorname{supp}\varphi\subset\mathscr{D}_{<l^{\prime}}
.
Let
\tilde{\chi}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};[0,1])
be a cut-off function satisfying

\begin{split}\operatorname{supp}\tilde{\chi}\subset(-\infty,l/2),\quad\tilde{\chi}((-\infty,l^{\prime}/2])=\{1\},\end{split}

(3.6)

\tilde{\chi}^{\varepsilon}:=\chi^{\varepsilon}\tilde{\chi}
, and
\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}:=\chi_{\varepsilon}\tilde{\chi}
. Then we decompose

\begin{split}(F*G)(\varphi)&=F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\tilde{\chi}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))+G_{\eta}F_{\xi}(\tilde{\chi}^{\varepsilon}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))\\
&\quad-F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\tilde{\chi}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1})\tilde{\chi}^{\varepsilon}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))\\
&\quad+F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))+G_{\eta}F_{\xi}(\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))\\
&\quad-F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1})\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta)),\end{split}

(3.7)

using that
(1-\tilde{\chi}(\xi_{1}))(1-\tilde{\chi}(\eta_{1}))\varphi(\xi+\eta)=0
by the support condition.
Since
2\varepsilon\leq\delta
and
\mu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}
imply
\mu(2\varepsilon)<1
, the first term is bounded by

\begin{split}|F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\tilde{\chi}^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))|&\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(F)\nu(2\varepsilon)\|G_{\eta}(\varphi(\xi+\eta))\|_{0}^{+}\\
&\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(F)\nu(2\varepsilon)\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(G)\nu(l)\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+}.\end{split}

(3.8)

By symmetry, the second term is bounded exactly in the same way,
while the third term is easier.
The fourth term is estimated similarly by

\begin{split}|F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))|&\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(F)\nu(l/2)\sup_{\xi_{1}>\varepsilon}|G_{\eta}(\varphi(\xi+\eta))|\\
&\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(F)\nu(l/2)\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(G)\nu(l-\varepsilon)\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+}.\end{split}

(3.9)

The fifth term is the same by symmetry, while the final term is bounded by

\begin{split}|F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1})\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))|&\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(F)\nu(l/2)\sup_{\xi_{1}>\varepsilon}|G_{\eta}(\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+y))|\\
&\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(F)\nu(l/2)\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(G)\nu(l-\varepsilon)\|\varphi\|_{0}^{+},\end{split}

(3.10)

since
l/2\leq 2\delta
implies
\mu(l/2)<1
. Thus we obtain

\begin{split}\tfrac{|F*G(\varphi)|}{\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(F)\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(G)}&\leq 3[\nu(2\varepsilon)\nu(l)+1_{l>2\varepsilon}\nu(l/2)\nu(l-\varepsilon)]\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+},\end{split}

(3.11)

where the restriction by
1_{l>2\varepsilon}
comes from
\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}=0
for
l\leq 2\varepsilon
.

For the other case
4\delta<l
, we replace the above
\tilde{\chi}_{\varepsilon}
with
\chi_{\varepsilon}^{\delta}:=\chi_{\varepsilon}\chi^{\delta}
and decompose

\begin{split}(F*G)(\varphi)&=F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\chi^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))+G_{\eta}F_{\xi}(\chi^{\varepsilon}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))\\
&\quad-F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\chi^{\varepsilon}(\xi_{1})\chi^{\varepsilon}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))\\
&\quad+F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\chi_{\varepsilon}^{\delta}(\xi_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))+G_{\eta}F_{\xi}(\chi_{\varepsilon}^{\delta}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))\\
&\quad-F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\chi_{\varepsilon}^{\delta}(\xi_{1})\chi_{\varepsilon}^{\delta}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))+F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\chi_{\delta}(\xi_{1})\chi_{\delta}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta)).\end{split}

(3.12)

The first six terms are estimated in the same way as in the previous case, using
\operatorname{supp}\chi_{\varepsilon}^{\delta}\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}_{(\varepsilon,2\delta)}
.
For the last remaining term, we have

\begin{split}l^{\prime}:=l-\delta\implies\operatorname{supp}\chi_{\delta}(\xi_{1})\chi_{\delta}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta)\subset\{\delta<\xi_{1},\eta_{1}<l^{\prime}\},\end{split}

(3.13)

and so

\begin{split}&|F_{\xi}G_{\eta}(\chi_{\delta}(\xi_{1})\chi_{\delta}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))|\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(F)\nu(l^{\prime})\|\chi_{\delta}(\xi_{1})G_{\eta}(\chi_{\delta}(\eta_{1})\varphi(\xi+\eta))\|_{\mu(l^{\prime})(\xi)}^{+}.\end{split}

(3.14)

The last norm is bounded by

\begin{split}&\max_{|\alpha|\leq\mu(l^{\prime})}\sup_{\delta<\xi_{1}<l^{\prime}}\sum_{0\leq k\leq\alpha_{1}}\tfrac{\alpha_{1}!}{(\alpha_{1}-k)!k!}|\chi_{\delta}^{(k)}(\xi_{1})G_{\eta}(\chi_{\delta}(\eta_{1})\partial^{\alpha-ke_{1}}\varphi(\xi+\eta))|\\
&\leq M_{\delta}^{\chi}(\mu(l^{\prime}))\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(G)\nu(l^{\prime})\sup_{\xi_{1}>\delta}\max_{|\alpha|\leq\mu(l^{\prime})}\|\chi_{\delta}(\eta_{1})\partial^{\alpha}\varphi(\xi+\eta)\|_{\mu(l^{\prime})(\eta)}^{+},\end{split}

(3.15)

where the last norm of (
3.15
) is bounded similarly by

M_{\delta}^{\chi}(\mu(l^{\prime}))\|\varphi\|_{2\mu(l^{\prime})}^{+}
,
and thus we obtain the desired estimate.
∎

As an immediate consequence of the above lemma, we have

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1]
,
\mu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}
,
\nu,\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
. Suppose

(1)

\mu\leq\tilde{\mu}
and
2\mu(l-\delta)\leq\tilde{\mu}(l)
for
l>4\delta
.

(2)

For each
l>0
there is
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta/2]
such that

9\nu(2\varepsilon)\nu(l)\leq\tilde{\nu}(l)
, and, if
2\varepsilon<l
then
9\nu(l-\varepsilon)^{2}\leq\tilde{\nu}(l)
.

(3)

For
l>4\delta
,

3[M_{\delta}^{\chi}(\mu(l-\delta))\nu(l-\delta)]^{2}\leq\tilde{\nu}(l)
.

Then we have for any
F,G\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
and
l\in(0,\infty)

\begin{split}\rho_{l}^{\tilde{\mu},\tilde{\nu}}(F*G)\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(F)\rho_{l}^{\mu,\nu}(G).\end{split}

(3.16)

Thus we have reduced the convolution estimate to the weight conditions.
Note that the condition on
\mu
is independent of
\nu,\tilde{\nu}
.
The point is that the left side for
\tilde{\nu}
has either a small factor or a smaller argument
l-\varepsilon
or
l-\delta
.
Hence, if
\mu,\nu
are accelerating sufficiently fast, then
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu}
becomes a convolution algebra, and the convolution may even gain arbitrary small factor.
More precisely, we have the following.

Lemma 3.4
.

For each
\delta\in(0,1]
, we have two mappings

\begin{split}&\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{1}:C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}\to C_{\delta}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}},\quad\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{2}:C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}\times C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}\times C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\searrow$}}}}}}_{(0,1]}\to C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}\end{split}

(3.17)

satisfying the following. Define
\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}:C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}\times C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}\times C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\searrow$}}}}}}_{(0,1]}\to C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}\times C_{0}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
by

\begin{split}\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}(\mu_{0},\nu_{0},\kappa):=(\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{1}(\mu_{0}),\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{2}(\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{1}(\mu_{0}),\nu_{0},\kappa)).\end{split}

(3.18)

Let

\mu_{1}:=\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{1}(\mu_{0})
and
\nu_{1}:=\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{2}(\mu_{0},\nu_{0},\kappa)
for any
\mu_{0},\nu_{0},\kappa
in the domain. Then

(1)

\mu_{0}\leq\mu_{1}
,
\nu_{0}\leq\kappa\nu_{1}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
.

(2)

\tilde{\mu}:=\mu:=\mu_{1}
satisfies the condition (1) of Lemma
3.3
.

(3)

\mu:=\mu_{0}
,
\nu:=\nu_{1}
and
\tilde{\nu}:=\kappa\nu_{1}
satisfy the conditions (2)-(3) of Lemma
3.3
. In particular, for
(\mu_{1},\nu_{1}):=\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}(\mu_{0},\nu_{0},\kappa)
,
F,G\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1}}
and
l\in(0,\infty)
we have

\begin{split}\rho_{l}^{\mu_{1},\kappa\nu_{1}}(F*G)\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1}}(F)\rho_{l}^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1}}(G).\end{split}

(3.19)

(4)

\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{1},\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{2}
are non-decreasing for the pointwise order.

(5)

\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{1},\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{2}
are continuous in the topology of locally uniform convergence.

(6)

Let
b\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{[1,\infty)}
satisfy
b(l)=b(0)
as long as
\nu_{0}(l)\leq\kappa
. Then
\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{2}(\mu_{0},b\nu_{0},\kappa)\geq b\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{2}(\mu_{0},\nu_{0},\kappa)
.

As a special case, for any
\mu_{0}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}
and
\nu_{0}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
, we obtain the Banach algebra for convolution:
\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1}}\supset\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{0},\nu_{0}}
by
(\mu_{1},\nu_{1}):=\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}(\mu_{0},\nu_{0},1)
.
Hence their union
\mathcal{X}
is also a convolution algebra.
In other words, the pointwise multiplication is continuous on
\mathcal{F}^{-1}\mathcal{X}
(after continuous extension).
A crucial point of the lemma for solving PDEs is that we may include arbitrary small factors
\kappa
.

Proof.

For any
\mu_{0}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}
,
\nu_{0}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
and
\kappa\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\searrow$}}}}}}_{(0,1]}
,
we will define
\mu_{1}=\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{1}(\mu_{0})
and
\nu_{1}=\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{2}(\mu_{0},\nu_{0},\kappa)
.
Henceforth, monotonicity and continuity for (vector) functions are for the pointwise ordering and for the local uniform convergence, respectively.

First we define
\mu_{1}
by

\begin{split}\mu_{1}(l):=2^{\max(l/\delta-3,0)}\mu_{0}(l).\end{split}

(3.20)

Then
\mu_{0}\leq\mu_{1}\in C_{\delta}^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
and for
l\geq 4\delta

\begin{split}2\mu_{1}(l-\delta)=2^{l/\delta-3}\mu_{0}(l-\delta)\leq 2^{l/\delta-3}\mu_{0}(l)=\mu_{1}(l).\end{split}

(3.21)

Moreover,
\mu_{0}\mapsto\mu_{1}
is non-decreasing and continuous.
Thus we have obtained
\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{1}
.

Next we define
\nu_{1}
. Let
\hat{\nu}:=\kappa^{-1}\nu_{0}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
.
The mapping
(\nu_{0},-\kappa)\mapsto\hat{\nu}
is non-decreasing and continuous.
For each
l>0
, let

\begin{split}\hat{\varepsilon}(l):=\sup\{\varepsilon>0\mid 9\hat{\nu}(2\varepsilon)\leq\kappa(l)(1-2\varepsilon/\delta)\}.\end{split}

(3.22)

Note that the set is non-empty because
\hat{\nu}(0)=0<\kappa(l)
.
The last factor
2\varepsilon/\delta
ensures
\hat{\varepsilon}(l)\leq\delta/2
, as well as the uniform decay in
\varepsilon
of the right hand side.
Hence
\hat{\varepsilon}:[0,\infty)\mapsto(0,\delta/2]
is non-increasing and continuous, so is the mapping
(\nu_{0},-\kappa)\mapsto\hat{\varepsilon}
.
Let

\begin{split}l_{0}:=\sup\{l>0\mid l\leq 2\hat{\varepsilon}(l)\}.\end{split}

(3.23)

Then
0<l_{0}\leq\delta
,
2\hat{\varepsilon}(l_{0})=l_{0}
,
t\leq 2\hat{\varepsilon}(t)
for
0<t\leq l_{0}
, and
0<2\hat{\varepsilon}(t)\leq l_{0}
for
t>l_{0}
, and
the mapping
\hat{\varepsilon}\mapsto l_{0}
is non-decreasing and continuous.
Let

\begin{split}\gamma(l):=l-\hat{\varepsilon}(l).\end{split}

(3.24)

Then
\gamma:[l_{0},\infty)\to[l_{0}/2,\infty)
is an increasing homeomorphism,
so is the inverse
\lambda:=\gamma^{-1}:[l_{0}/2,\infty)\to[l_{0},\infty)
.
The mapping
\hat{\varepsilon}\mapsto\lambda
is non-decreasing and continuous.

To satisfy the condition (2) of Lemma
3.3
for
\nu=\nu_{1}
and
\tilde{\nu}=\kappa\nu_{1}
,
we choose

\begin{split}\varepsilon:=\min(\tfrac{l_{0}}{2},\hat{\varepsilon}(l)).\end{split}

(3.25)

Define
\nu_{1}
first on
[0,l_{0}]
by
\nu_{1}(t):=\hat{\nu}(t)
.
Since
2\varepsilon\leq l_{0}
and
\varepsilon\leq\hat{\varepsilon}(l)
, we have

\begin{split}9\nu(2\varepsilon)\nu(l)=9\hat{\nu}(2\varepsilon)\nu_{1}(l)\leq 9\hat{\nu}(2\hat{\varepsilon}(l))\nu_{1}(l)\leq\kappa(l)\nu_{1}(l),\end{split}

(3.26)

by the monotonicity of
\hat{\nu}
and the definition of
\hat{\varepsilon}
.
Then it remains to satisfy

\begin{split}&l>l_{0}\implies 9\nu_{1}(l-\hat{\varepsilon}(l))^{2}\leq\kappa(l)\nu_{1}(l),\\
&l>4\delta\implies 3[M_{\delta}^{\chi}(\mu_{0}(l-\delta))\nu_{1}(l-\delta)]^{2}\leq\kappa(l)\nu_{1}(l),\end{split}

(3.27)

as well as monotonicity of
\kappa\nu_{1}
, to apply Lemma
3.3
.

We define
\nu_{1}
to satisfy them iteratively starting from
\nu_{1}=\hat{\nu}
on
[0,l_{0}]
which is already fixed above. Define the sequence
l_{0}<l_{1}<\cdots
by

\begin{split}l_{n}:=\lambda(l_{n-1})\iff l_{n-1}=\gamma(l_{n})=l_{n}-\hat{\varepsilon}(l_{n}).\end{split}

(3.28)

Since
\hat{\varepsilon}
is positive and continuous, this sequence diverges
l_{n}\nearrow\infty
as
n\to\infty
(otherwise
\hat{\varepsilon}(l_{n})\to 0
would contradict).
Then we define
\nu_{1}
on
(l_{n-1},l_{n}]
inductively by

\begin{split}&\nu_{1}(l):=\mathscr{M}(\hat{\nu}(l),\max(\nu_{L}(l),\nu_{H}(l))),\quad\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta):=\max(\alpha,\beta,\alpha\beta),\\
&\quad\nu_{L}(l):=\tfrac{9\nu_{1}(\gamma(l))^{2}}{\kappa(l)},\quad\nu_{H}(l):=1_{l>3\delta}\tfrac{3[M_{\delta}^{\chi}(\mu_{0}(l-\delta))\nu_{1}(l-\delta)]^{2}}{\kappa(l)}.\end{split}

(3.29)

Since
l_{n}-l_{n-1}=\hat{\varepsilon}(l_{n})<\delta
, the right hand side of (
3.29
) is well-defined if
\nu_{1}
is already defined on
[0,l_{n-1}]
.
Thus
\nu_{1}:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)
is well-defined.
Since
\mathscr{M}(\alpha,\beta)\geq\max(\alpha,\beta)
, we have
\nu_{1}\geq\max(\hat{\nu},\nu_{L},\nu_{H})
,
which implies (
3.27
) and
\kappa\nu_{1}\geq\nu_{0}
.
In fact, we could define
\nu_{1}=\max(\hat{\nu},\nu_{L},\nu_{H})
to satisfy the properties (1)–(5),
but the factor
\alpha\beta
of
\mathscr{M}
is needed for the superlinear property (6).
The monotonicity of
\nu_{1},\kappa\nu_{1}
is also immediate by induction.

Similarly for the continuity, it suffices to check it at
l=l_{0}
and
3\delta
.
Around
l=l_{0}<3\delta
, we have
\nu_{H}=0
and
\nu_{L}<\hat{\nu}
, because
\nu_{1}=\hat{\nu}
for
l\leq l_{0}
and

\begin{split}9\hat{\nu}(\gamma(l_{0}))^{2}\leq 9\hat{\nu}(l_{0})^{2}\leq\kappa(l_{0})(1-l_{0}/\delta)\hat{\nu}(l_{0}).\end{split}

(3.30)

Hence
\nu_{1}=\hat{\nu}
around
l=l_{0}
, which is continuous.
Around
l=3\delta
, we have
\nu_{H}<\nu_{L}
, because

\begin{split}3[M_{\delta}^{\chi}(\mu_{0}(2\delta))\nu_{1}(2\delta)]^{2}<9\nu_{1}(2\delta)^{2}<9\nu_{1}(\gamma(3\delta))^{2},\end{split}

(3.31)

where we used
\mu_{0}(2\delta)\leq 1/2
,
M_{\delta}^{\chi}(1/2)^{2}<3

and
\hat{\varepsilon}(3\delta)<\delta
. Hence the definition of
\nu_{1}
is the same as the case of
l<3\delta
around
l=3\delta
, which is continuous.
Thus we conclude
\nu_{1},\kappa\nu_{1}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
.
The monotonicity and continuity of the mapping
(\mu_{0},\hat{\nu},-\kappa)\mapsto\nu_{1}
follows also by induction.

Thus we have defined the mapping
\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}
satisfying the desired properties (1)–(5).

It remains to check the final (6), namely the superlinearity in
\nu
for multiplication.
We follow the above procedure for the new input
(\mu_{0},b\nu_{0},\kappa)
, distinguishing the modified output with subscript
\bullet
.
We have
\hat{\nu}_{\bullet}=b\hat{\nu}\geq\hat{\nu}
.
Then
\hat{\varepsilon}_{\bullet}\leq\hat{\varepsilon}
,
l_{0\bullet}\leq l_{0}
,
\gamma_{\bullet}\geq\gamma
and
\lambda_{\bullet}\leq\lambda
, by their mapping monotonicity.
Hence by induction
l_{n\bullet}\leq l_{n}
for all
n\geq 0
.

Let
l_{*}:=\sup\hat{\nu}^{-1}([0,1])
.
Then by the assumption,
b\geq 1
is a constant on
l<l_{*}
, where
\nu_{1\bullet}\geq b\nu_{1}
follows by induction.
For
l\geq l_{*}
,
\hat{\nu}_{0}\geq 1
implies

\begin{split}\nu_{1}=\mathscr{M}(\hat{\nu},\max(\nu_{L},\nu_{H}))=\hat{\nu}\max(1,\nu_{L},\nu_{H}),\end{split}

(3.32)

hence

\begin{split}\nu_{1\bullet}=\hat{\nu}_{\bullet}\max(1,\nu_{L\bullet},\nu_{H\bullet})=b\hat{\nu}\max(1,\nu_{L\bullet},\nu_{H\bullet}),\end{split}

(3.33)

so
\nu_{1\bullet}\geq b\nu_{1}
for
l\geq l_{*}
follows from the monotonicity of
\nu_{L},\nu_{H}
.
∎

The convolution estimate in
\mathcal{X}
is transferred to
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu,\iota}_{\partial}
by

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
\mu_{k},\nu_{k}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
for
k=0,1,2
such that for all
F,G\in\mathcal{X}
and
l\in(0,\infty)
we have

\rho_{l}^{\mu_{0},\nu_{0}}(F*G)\leq\rho_{l}^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1}}(F)\rho_{l}^{\mu_{2},\nu_{2}}(G)
.
Then for any
\kappa\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}
, we have

\begin{split}\|F*G\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{0},\nu_{0},\kappa\nu_{0}}_{\partial}}\leq\|F\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1},\kappa\nu_{1}}_{\partial}}\|G\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{2},\nu_{2}}}.\end{split}

(3.34)

Proof.

Let
F\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1},\kappa\nu_{1}}_{\partial}
and
F=F_{0}+\partial_{1}F_{1}+\cdots+\partial_{d}F_{d}
for some
F_{0}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1}}
and
F_{k}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{1},\kappa\nu_{1}}
(
k\geq 1
). Then, we have
F*G=F_{0}*G+\sum_{1\leq k\leq d}\partial_{k}(F_{k}*G)
with

\begin{split}&\|F_{0}*G\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{0},\nu_{0}}}\leq\|F_{0}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{1},\nu_{1}}}\|G\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{2},\nu_{2}}},\\
&\|F_{k}*G\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{0},\kappa\nu_{0}}}\leq\|F_{k}\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{1},\kappa\nu_{1}}}\|G\|_{\mathcal{X}^{\mu_{2},\nu_{2}}},\end{split}

(3.35)

by using Lemma
2.2
. The infimum for
\{F_{k}\}_{0\leq k\leq d}
yields the conclusion.
∎

4.
Global wellposedness for distributions

Consider the Taylor expansion of
\mathscr{H}=(\mathscr{H}_{1},\ldots,\mathscr{H}_{n_{3}})
at
z=0\in\mathbb{C}^{n_{2}}

\begin{split}\mathscr{H}_{k}(z)=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n_{2}},\ |\alpha|\geq 2}\tfrac{1}{\alpha!}\mathscr{H}_{k}^{(\alpha)}(0)z^{\alpha}\quad(k=1,\ldots,n_{3}),\end{split}

(4.1)

which is assumed to be absolutely convergent in a
0
-neighborhood in
\mathbb{C}^{n_{2}}
: There exists
R_{\mathscr{H}}>0
such that for all
r<R_{\mathscr{H}}
, we have

\begin{split}\tilde{\mathscr{H}}(r):=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n_{2}},|\alpha|\geq 2}\tfrac{1}{\alpha!}\|\mathscr{H}^{(\alpha)}(0)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n_{3}}}r^{|\alpha|-2}<\infty.\end{split}

(4.2)

Note that
\mathscr{H}(0)=0
is crucial for the global wellposedness, since otherwise
\mathscr{H}
could produce
0
frequency in the solution, leading to ODE blow-up in general (cf. Section
6.1
).
On the other hand,
\mathscr{H}^{\prime}(0)=0
is not an essential restriction, because the linear part may be included in
L
.

The difference of
\mathscr{H}
between two points may be estimated using

\begin{split}\tilde{\mathscr{H}}^{(1)}(r):=\sum_{1\leq j\leq n_{2}}\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n_{2}},|\alpha|\geq 1}\tfrac{1}{\alpha!}\|\mathscr{H}^{(\alpha+e_{j})}(0)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n_{3}}}r^{|\alpha|-1},\end{split}

(4.3)

which is also finite for
r<R_{\mathscr{H}}
.
The Fourier transform (in
x
) of equation (
1.1
) is written for
\hat{u}:=\mathcal{F}u
as

\begin{split}&\hat{u}_{t}=\hat{L}\hat{u}+\hat{N}\hat{\mathscr{H}}(\hat{M}\hat{u}),\quad\hat{\mathscr{H}}_{k}(v):=\sum_{\alpha\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{n_{2}}}\tfrac{1}{\alpha!}\mathscr{H}_{k}^{(\alpha)}(0)v^{*\alpha},\quad\hat{u}(0)=\hat{u}_{0},\end{split}

(4.4)

where
v^{*\alpha}
denotes the multi-index power in convolution, defined by

\begin{split}v^{*\alpha}=v_{1}^{*\alpha_{1}}*\cdots*v_{n_{2}}^{*\alpha_{n_{2}}},\quad v_{1}^{*(a+1)}=v_{1}^{*a}*v_{1},\quad v_{1}^{*0}=\delta,\end{split}

(4.5)

and the Duhamel form is

\begin{split}\hat{u}(t)=\mathcal{D}(\hat{u}_{0},\hat{u}):=e^{t\hat{L}(\xi)}\hat{u}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(t-s)\hat{L}(\xi)}\hat{N}(\xi)\hat{\mathscr{H}}(\hat{M}(\xi)\hat{u}(s))ds.\end{split}

(4.6)

Note that the right side is closed on
C(\mathbb{R};\mathcal{X})
.
The idea is to solve it by the fixed point theorem using some norm on
C([0,\infty);\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu})
for appropriate choice of
\mu,\nu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
, where
\nu
depends on
t\geq 0
.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}
satisfying
2\mu(l-\delta)\leq\mu(l)
for
l>4\delta
.
If
\mu_{0}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}
is given, then we may choose
\mu:=\mathfrak{C}_{\delta}^{1}(\mu_{0})\geq\mu_{0}
by Lemma
3.4
.
For the coefficients
\hat{L},\hat{M},\hat{N}
,
assume that they are smooth on
\mathbb{R}^{d}_{>0}
and that
there exist
\theta\in[0,1)
and
c,\tilde{L},\tilde{M}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{[1,\infty)}
satisfying for all
t,l>0
,
\varphi\in(\mathscr{D}_{<l})^{n_{1}}
, and
\psi\in(\mathscr{D}_{<l})^{n_{2}}
,

\begin{split}&\|e^{t\hat{L}^{\intercal}(\xi)}\varphi\|_{\mu(l),\delta}+\|t^{\theta}\hat{N}^{\intercal}(\xi)e^{t\hat{L}^{\intercal}(\xi)}\varphi\|_{\mu(l),\delta}\leq c(l)e^{t\tilde{L}(l)}\|\varphi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+},\\
&\|\hat{M}^{\intercal}(\xi)\psi\|_{\mu(l),\delta}\leq\tilde{M}(l)\|\psi\|_{\mu(l)}^{+}.\end{split}

(4.7)

The factor
t^{\theta}
is to exploit smoothing effect of
e^{t\hat{L}}
when it is available.
Lemma
2.11
implies that
(C-s)
with
\mathrm{v}=e_{1}
is a sufficient condition for the above to hold for all
\mu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
.
In particular, it is satisfied with
\theta=0
if all components of
\hat{L},\hat{M},\hat{N}
are in
Y
defined in (
2.59
). For
d=1
, it is enough that
\hat{L},\hat{M},\hat{N}
are smooth on
\xi_{1}>0
and bounded as
\xi_{1}\to+0
.

Now fix
\nu_{0}\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
, and let
l_{*}:=\sup\nu_{0}^{-1}([0,1])
.
Without losing generality, we may assume
l_{*}<\infty
(otherwise we may multiply
\nu_{0}
with a big constant without changing the space
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu_{0}}
).
Then we may replace
\tilde{L}
in (
4.7
) by a larger function if necessary such that

\tilde{L}(l)
is a constant for
0<l<l_{*}
.

Fix
b_{0}>0
and the initial data
\hat{u}_{0}\in\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu_{0}}
such that
\rho^{\mu,\nu_{0}}(\hat{u}_{0})\leq b_{0}
.
For any
\nu_{1}\in C([0,\infty);C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0})
,
\hat{u}\in C([0,\infty);\mathcal{X}^{n_{1}})
and
l>0
, we obtain from (
2.10
) and (
4.7
)

\begin{split}\rho^{\mu(l)}_{l}(\mathcal{D}(\hat{u}_{0},\hat{u}))&\leq c(l)e^{t\tilde{L}(l)}\nu_{0}(l)\rho^{\mu,\nu_{0}}_{l}(\hat{u}_{0})\\
&\quad+\int_{0}^{t}c(l)(t-s)^{-\theta}e^{(t-s)\tilde{L}(l)}\nu_{1}(s,l)\rho^{\mu,\nu_{1}(s)}_{l}(\hat{\mathscr{H}}(\hat{M}(\xi)\hat{u}(s)))ds.\end{split}

(4.8)

Let
b_{1}:=2b_{0}
and fix
\varepsilon>0
small enough so that

\begin{split}b_{1}\varepsilon<R_{\mathscr{H}},\quad b_{1}\varepsilon\tilde{\mathscr{H}}(b_{1}\varepsilon)\Gamma(1-\theta)\leq 1/2,\quad b_{1}\varepsilon\tilde{\mathscr{H}}^{(1)}(b_{1}\varepsilon)\Gamma(1-\theta)\leq 1/2.\end{split}

(4.9)

Define
\nu_{*},\nu_{1},\nu_{2},\nu:[0,\infty)\to C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
and
\kappa\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\searrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\searrow$}}}}}}_{(0,1]}
by using Lemma
3.4

\begin{split}&\nu_{*}(t,l):=\varepsilon^{-1}\nu_{0}(l)e^{t(1+\tilde{L}(l))},\quad\kappa:=1/(c\tilde{M}),\\
&\nu_{2}(t):=\mathfrak{C}^{2}_{\delta}(\mu,\nu_{*}(t),\kappa),\quad\nu_{1}:=\kappa\nu_{2},\quad\nu(t):=\varepsilon\nu_{2}(t)/\tilde{M}=\varepsilon c\nu_{1}(t).\end{split}

(4.10)

At any fixed
t\geq 0
, let
v\in\mathcal{X}^{n_{1}}
and
V:=\max_{1\leq j\leq n_{2}}\rho^{\mu,\nu_{2}}(v_{j})\leq\rho^{\mu,\nu_{2}}(v)
. If
V<R_{\mathscr{H}}
then repeated use of (
3.19
) to the Taylor expansion of
\hat{\mathscr{H}}
yields

\begin{split}\rho^{\mu,\nu_{1}}(\hat{\mathscr{H}}(v))\leq\sum_{|\alpha|\geq 2}\tfrac{1}{\alpha!}\|\mathscr{H}^{(\alpha)}(0)\|_{\mathbb{C}^{n_{3}}}V^{|\alpha|}\leq\tilde{\mathscr{H}}(V)V^{2}.\end{split}

(4.11)

Note that every summand contains convolution as
|\alpha|\geq 2
.
The difference for any
v^{0},v^{1}\in\mathcal{X}^{n_{1}}
is estimated in the same way as above,

\begin{split}&\tilde{V}:=\max_{k=0,1}\max_{j=1,\ldots,n_{2}}\rho^{\mu,\nu_{2}}(v^{k}_{j})<R_{\mathscr{H}}\\
&\implies\rho^{\mu,\nu_{1}}(\hat{\mathscr{H}}(v^{0})-\hat{\mathscr{H}}(v^{1}))\leq\tilde{\mathscr{H}}^{(1)}(\tilde{V})\tilde{V}\rho^{\mu,\nu_{2}}(v^{0}-v^{1}).\end{split}

(4.12)

Since
\tilde{M}\nu\leq\varepsilon\nu_{2}
, we have, again using (
2.10
) and (
4.7
)

\begin{split}\rho^{\mu,\nu_{2}}(\hat{M}\hat{u})\leq\varepsilon\rho^{\mu,\nu}(\hat{u}),\end{split}

(4.13)

and, injecting it into (
4.11
),

\begin{split}\rho^{\mu,\nu_{1}}(\hat{\mathscr{H}}(\hat{M}\hat{u}))\leq\tilde{\mathscr{H}}(\varepsilon\rho^{\mu,\nu}(\hat{u}))|\varepsilon\rho^{\mu,\nu}(\hat{u})|^{2},\end{split}

(4.14)

for all
t\geq 0
, provided that
\varepsilon\rho^{\mu,\nu}(\hat{u})<R_{\mathscr{H}}
.
Injecting this into (
4.8
), we obtain

\begin{split}&\rho^{\mu(l)}_{l}(\mathcal{D}(\hat{u}_{0},\hat{u}))\leq c(l)e^{t\tilde{L}(l)}\nu_{0}(l)b_{0}\\
&\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}c(l)(t-s)^{-\theta}e^{(t-s)\tilde{L}(l)}\nu_{1}(s,l)\tilde{\mathscr{H}}(\varepsilon\rho^{\mu,\nu(s)}(\hat{u}(s)))|\varepsilon\rho^{\mu,\nu(s)}(\hat{u}(s))|^{2}ds.\end{split}

(4.15)

Suppose that
\rho^{\mu,\nu(t)}(\hat{u}(t))\leq b_{1}
for all
t\geq 0
.
Since
\nu=\varepsilon c\nu_{1}\geq\varepsilon c\nu_{*}\geq c\nu_{0}e^{t\tilde{L}}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\rho^{\mu,\nu(t)}(\mathcal{D}(\hat{u}_{0},\hat{u}(t)))\leq b_{0}+\varepsilon\tilde{\mathscr{H}}(\varepsilon b_{1})b_{1}^{2}\sup_{l>0}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\theta}e^{s-t}\tfrac{\tilde{\nu}_{1}(s,l)}{\tilde{\nu}_{1}(t,l)}ds,\end{split}

(4.16)

where

\begin{split}\tilde{\nu}_{1}(t,l):=e^{-t(1+\tilde{L}(l))}\nu_{1}(t,l).\end{split}

(4.17)

Now the superlinear property (6) of
\mathfrak{C}^{2}_{\delta}
in Lemma
3.4
implies

\begin{split}\tfrac{\tilde{\nu}_{1}(s)}{\tilde{\nu}_{1}(t)}=e^{(t-s)(1+\tilde{L})}\tfrac{\mathfrak{C}^{2}_{\delta}(\mu,\nu_{*}(s),\kappa)}{\mathfrak{C}^{2}_{\delta}(\mu,\nu_{*}(t),\kappa)}\leq\tfrac{\mathfrak{C}^{2}_{\delta}(\mu,e^{(t-s)(1+\tilde{L})}\nu_{*}(s),\kappa)}{\mathfrak{C}^{2}_{\delta}(\mu,\nu_{*}(t),\kappa)}=1,\end{split}

(4.18)

where we used that
\tilde{L}
is constant on
[0,l^{*}]
and
\nu_{*}(s,l^{*})\geq\nu_{0}(l^{*})=1
.
Thus we obtain

\begin{split}\sup_{t\geq 0}\rho^{\mu,\nu(t)}(\hat{u}(t))\leq b_{1}\implies\rho^{\mu,\nu}(\mathcal{D}(\hat{u}_{0},\hat{u}))\leq b_{0}+\varepsilon\tilde{\mathscr{H}}(\varepsilon b_{1})b_{1}^{2}\Gamma(1-\theta),\end{split}

(4.19)

and also

\begin{split}&\max_{k=0,1}\sup_{t\geq 0}\rho^{\mu,\nu}(\hat{u}^{k})\leq b_{1}\implies\\
&\quad\sup_{t\geq 0}\rho^{\mu,\nu}(\mathcal{D}(\hat{u}_{0},\hat{u}^{0})-\mathcal{D}(\hat{u}_{0},\hat{u}^{1}))\leq\varepsilon b_{1}\tilde{\mathscr{H}}^{(1)}(\varepsilon b_{1})\Gamma(1-\theta)\sup_{t\geq 0}\rho^{\mu,\nu}(\hat{u}^{0}-\hat{u}^{1}).\end{split}

(4.20)

Hence by the choice of
\varepsilon
in (
4.9
),
\hat{u}\mapsto\mathcal{D}(\hat{u}_{0},\hat{u})
is a contraction map on the complete metric space

\begin{split}\{\hat{u}\in C([0,\infty);\mathcal{X}^{n_{1}})\mid\sup_{t\geq 0}\rho^{\mu,\nu(t)}(\hat{u}(t))\leq b_{1}=2b_{0}\}\end{split}

(4.21)

for the metric
\sup_{t\geq 0}\rho^{\mu,\nu}
, whose fixed point is a unique global solution of (
4.6
). For any
T>0
, the integral in (
4.6
) may be defined as the Bochner integral in
\mathcal{X}^{\mu,\nu_{T}}
for
t<T
, for
\nu_{T}:=\min_{0\leq t\leq T}\nu(t)\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{0}
, as well as the Riemann integral in
\mathcal{X}
.
In this sense, (
4.6
) is equivalent to (
4.4
).
Thus we obtain a unique global solution
u
of (
1.1
) in the inverse Fourier transform of the above space (
4.21
).

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
\delta\in(0,1]
and
\mu\in C^{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\displaystyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552668}{262143}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.3611pt}{$\textstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{552845}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.95276pt}{$\scriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.11998pt}{\resizebox{622990}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.68054pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\nearrow$}}}}}}_{\delta}
satisfy
2\mu(l-\delta)\leq\mu(l)
for
l>4\delta
.
Suppose that there exist
\theta\in[0,1)
and
